```html
  ({ '&':'&amp;', '<':'&lt;', '>':'&gt;', '"':'&quot;', "'":'&#39;' }[c]));

function initials(name) {

function avatar(u, size = 24) {
  return `<span class="av" style="--c:${u.color};width:${size}px;height:${size}px;font-size:${Math.round(size*0.42)}px" title="${esc(u.name)}">${initials(u.name)}</span>`;

function relTime(ts) {
  const m = Math.floor((Date.now() - ts) / 60000);
  if (m < 1) return 'just now';
  if (m < 60) return m + 'm ago';
  const h = Math.floor(m / 60);
  if (h < 24) return h + 'h ago';
  const d = Math.floor(h / 24);
  if (d < 7) return d + 'd ago';
  return new Date(ts).toLocaleDateString(undefined, { month:'short', day:'numeric' });
}
function preview(body) {
  return body.split('\n')
    .map(l => l.replace(/^[\s>#\-*\d.]+/, '').replace(/[*`_[\]]/g, ''))
    .filter(Boolean).join(' ').slice(0, 140);
}

  return esc(s)
    .replace(/`([^`]+)`/g, '<code>$1</code>')
    .replace(/\*\*([^*]+)\*\*/g, '<strong>$1</strong>')
    .replace(/(^|[^\w*])\*([^*\n]+)\*/g, '$1<em>$2</em>')
    .replace(/(^|[^\w_])_([^_\n]+)_/g, '$1<em>$2</em>');
}
```

### How It Works & Key Interactions

The page behaves like a small desktop app, so the main things to know are how to navigate, edit, and interact with notes.

- **Navigation and filtering:** The left sidebar shows smart lists (All notes, My notes, Shared with me, Mentions, Pinned) and a tag list. The middle column shows a filtered, searchable list. Clicking a note opens it in the editor pane on the right.
- **Creating and editing:** Use the + button or the keyboard shortcut to create a new note. The title and body are editable inline. Markdown-style formatting (headings, lists, checkboxes, blockquotes, inline code, links) renders as you type.
- **Task checkboxes:** Checkbox items in a note can be toggled directly in the preview. The underlying markdown updates, and the note is saved.
- **Comments:** Each note has a comment thread at the bottom. You can add comments as the current user, and comments use the same lightweight markdown rendering.
- **Auto-save and persistence:** Edits are saved to localStorage with a short debounce, so your changes survive a page refresh without needing a backend.
- **Keyboard shortcuts:** Press ⌘K (or Ctrl+K) to focus search. Escape closes the editor or clears focus.
---

**Optimization Tip:** The app uses static sample data inside the `seed()` function and stores edits in your browser's localStorage. To connect it to a real backend, you can replace the `scheduleSave` function and the seed data with API calls that fetch, create, update, and delete notes.

Output

Building a collaborative notes app for teams requires three core pillars: **Nested Document Hierarchy**, **Real-Time Multiplayer Collaboration (CRDTs)**, and **Role-Based Access Control (RBAC)**.

Here is an end-to-end blueprint and a working implementation using **Next.js (App Router)**, **Tailwind CSS**, **TipTap (ProseMirror)**, and **Yjs** for real-time multiplayer editing.

---

### 1. System Architecture

```
                    ┌─────────────────────────┐
                    │  Next.js Frontend (UI)  │
                    │   TipTap + Yjs Provider │
                    └────────────┬────────────┘
                                 │
                 ┌───────────────┴───────────────┐
                 ▼                               ▼
    ┌─────────────────────────┐     ┌─────────────────────────┐
    │     Supabase / Auth     │     │   Y-WebSockets Server   │
    │ PostgreSQL (Relational) │     │ (Live Cursors & State)  │
    └─────────────────────────┘     └─────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 2. Database Schema (PostgreSQL / Supabase)

Run this SQL migration to set up the workspace, memberships, and document structure with recursive parent-child nesting.

```sql
-- Workspaces / Teams
CREATE TABLE workspaces (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    name TEXT NOT NULL,
    slug TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Workspace Members & Roles
CREATE TYPE user_role AS ENUM ('owner', 'admin', 'member', 'viewer');

CREATE TABLE workspace_members (
    workspace_id UUID REFERENCES workspaces(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    user_id UUID NOT NULL,
    role user_role DEFAULT 'member',
    PRIMARY KEY (workspace_id, user_id)
);

-- Nested Documents
CREATE TABLE documents (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    workspace_id UUID REFERENCES workspaces(id) ON DELETE CASCADE NOT NULL,
    parent_id UUID REFERENCES documents(id) ON DELETE CASCADE, -- Allows folder-like nesting
    title TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'Untitled',
    content JSONB, -- Fallback snapshot for search indexing
    created_by UUID NOT NULL,
    is_archived BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Indexing for fast search and sidebar queries
CREATE INDEX idx_documents_workspace ON documents(workspace_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_documents_parent ON documents(parent_id);
```

---

### 3. Frontend Implementation

#### Install Dependencies
```bash
npm install @tiptap/react @tiptap/starter-kit @tiptap/extension-collaboration @tiptap/extension-collaboration-cursor yjs y-webrtc lucide-react clsx tailwind-merge
```

#### Document Editor with Multiplayer Awareness (`Editor.tsx`)
This component uses **Yjs** and **WebRTC** (for peer-to-peer sync without needing a custom server immediately) to render active multiplayer cursors.

```tsx
'use client';

import { useEditor, EditorContent } from '@tiptap/react';
import StarterKit from '@tiptap/starter-kit';
import Collaboration from '@tiptap/extension-collaboration';
import CollaborationCursor from '@tiptap/extension-collaboration-cursor';
import * as Y from 'yjs';
import { WebrtcProvider } from 'y-webrtc';
import { useEffect, useMemo, useState } from 'react';

// Random color generator for user cursors
const USER_COLORS = ['#f87171', '#fb923c', '#fbbf24', '#34d399', '#60a5fa', '#a78bfa'];
const getRandomColor = () => USER_COLORS[Math.floor(Math.random() * USER_COLORS.length)];

interface EditorProps {
  documentId: string;
  currentUser: { id: string; name: string };
}

export default function CollaborativeEditor({ documentId, currentUser }: EditorProps) {
  const ydoc = useMemo(() => new Y.Doc(), [documentId]);
  const [status, setStatus] = useState<'connecting' | 'connected'>('connecting');

  // Initialize Provider (Using WebRTC room for immediate local/network sync)
  const provider = useMemo(() => {
    return new WebrtcProvider(`team-notes-${documentId}`, ydoc, {
      signaling: ['wss://signaling.yjs.dev'], // Public demo signaling; self-host in production
    });
  }, [documentId, ydoc]);

  const userColor = useMemo(() => getRandomColor(), []);

  const editor = useEditor({
    extensions: [
      StarterKit.configure({ history: false }), // History must be disabled when using Yjs
      Collaboration.configure({
        document: ydoc,
      }),
      CollaborationCursor.configure({
        provider,
        user: {
          name: currentUser.name,
          color: userColor,
        },
      }),
    ],
  });

  useEffect(() => {
    provider.on('status', (event: { status: 'connecting' | 'connected' }) => {
      setStatus(event.status);
    });

    return () => {
      provider.destroy();
      ydoc.destroy();
    };
  }, [provider, ydoc]);

  return (
    <div className="flex flex-col h-full w-full max-w-4xl mx-auto px-4">
      {/* Editor Status Bar */}
      <div className="flex items-center justify-between border-b py-3 text-xs text-gray-500">
        <div className="flex items-center gap-2">
          <span
            className={`h-2 w-2 rounded-full ${
              status === 'connected' ? 'bg-emerald-500' : 'bg-amber-500'
            }`}
          />
          <span className="capitalize">{status}</span>
        </div>
        <div className="text-gray-400">Edits sync in real-time across your team</div>
      </div>

      {/* Title Placeholder */}
      <input
        type="text"
        placeholder="Untitled Note"
        className="w-full text-4xl font-bold mt-8 mb-4 outline-none bg-transparent placeholder-gray-300"
      />

      {/* The Text Surface */}
      <div className="prose prose-stone dark:prose-invert max-w-none focus:outline-none flex-1">
        <EditorContent editor={editor} />
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}
```

### 4. Team Workspace Shell Layout (`AppLayout.tsx`)

Build the collaborative navigation frame: sidebar with document tree, team-switcher, and active document workspace.

```tsx
'use client';

import React, { useState } from 'react';
import { 
  FileText, Plus, Users, Hash, ChevronRight, Settings, Search 
} from 'lucide-react';
import CollaborativeEditor from './Editor';

interface DocItem {
  id: string;
  title: string;
}

export default function WorkspaceApp() {
  const [documents, setDocuments] = useState<DocItem[]>([
    { id: '1', title: 'Product Roadmap 2025' },
    { id: '2', title: 'Engineering RFC: API Specs' },
    { id: '3', title: 'Weekly Sync Notes' },
  ]);
  const [activeDocId, setActiveDocId] = useState<string>('1');

  const addDocument = () => {
    const newDoc: DocItem = {
      id: String(Date.now()),
      title: 'Untitled Note',
    };
    setDocuments([...documents, newDoc]);
    setActiveDocId(newDoc.id);
  };

  return (
    <div className="flex h-screen w-screen overflow-hidden bg-white antialiased">
      {/* Sidebar Navigation */}
      <aside className="w-64 border-r border-gray-200 bg-gray-50 flex flex-col justify-between">
        <div>
          {/* Team Switcher Header */}
          <div className="h-14 border-b border-gray-200 px-4 flex items-center justify-between">
            <div className="flex items-center gap-2">
              <div className="h-6 w-6 rounded bg-black text-white flex items-center justify-center font-bold text-xs">
                A
              </div>
              <span className="font-semibold text-sm">Acme Corp</span>
            </div>
            <Users className="w-4 h-4 text-gray-400 cursor-pointer" />
          </div>

          {/* Search Action */}
          <div className="p-3">
            <button className="flex w-full items-center gap-2 px-2 py-1.5 text-xs text-gray-500 bg-gray-100 hover:bg-gray-200 rounded border border-gray-200 transition">
              <Search className="w-3.5 h-3.5" />
              <span>Search workspace...</span>
            </button>
          </div>

          {/* Document Tree */}
          <div className="px-2">
            <div className="flex items-center justify-between px-2 py-1 text-xs font-semibold text-gray-400 tracking-wider">
              <span>NOTES</span>
              <button onClick={addDocument} className="hover:bg-gray-200 rounded p-0.5">
                <Plus className="w-4 h-4 text-gray-600" />
              </button>
            </div>

            <div className="mt-1 space-y-0.5">
              {documents.map((doc) => (
                <button
                  key={doc.id}
                  onClick={() => setActiveDocId(doc.id)}
                  className={`w-full flex items-center gap-2 px-2 py-1.5 text-sm rounded-md text-left transition ${
                    activeDocId === doc.id
                      ? 'bg-gray-200 font-medium text-gray-900'
                      : 'text-gray-600 hover:bg-gray-100'
                  }`}
                >
                  <FileText className="w-4 h-4 text-gray-400 shrink-0" />
                  <span className="truncate">{doc.title}</span>
                </button>
              ))}
            </div>
          </div>
        </div>

        {/* Workspace Footer */}
        <div className="p-3 border-t border-gray-200 flex items-center justify-between text-xs text-gray-500">
          <div className="flex items-center gap-2">
            <div className="w-6 h-6 rounded-full bg-blue-500 text-white flex items-center justify-center text-[10px]">
              JD
            </div>
            <span className="font-medium text-gray-700">John Doe</span>
          </div>
          <Settings className="w-4 h-4 cursor-pointer hover:text-black" />
        </div>
      </aside>

      {/* Note Surface */}
      <main className="flex-1 overflow-y-auto">
        <CollaborativeEditor
          documentId={activeDocId}
          currentUser={{ id: 'usr-1', name: 'John Doe' }}
        />
      </main>
    </div>
  );
}
```

---

### 5. Production Real-Time Architecture (Scaling Beyond WebRTC)

While the sample code above leverages WebRTC for fast setup, production applications (like Notion or Linear) require an authoritative server to persist changes and resolve connection boundaries.

1. **Self-Host `y-websocket` Server**:
   Run a Node.js daemon using the standard Yjs WebSocket provider:
   ```bash
   npx y-websocket
   ```
2. **PostgreSQL Persistence Extension**:
   Hook the WebSocket server to PostgreSQL by listening to updates via the `y-leveldb` or direct byte writes:
   ```javascript
   import { setupWSConnection } from 'y-websocket/bin/utils';
   import * as Y from 'yjs';

   // On connection: Fetch saved binary state from PostgreSQL
   // On document update: Debounce write changes back to Postgres as BYTEA
   ```
3. **Cursor Styling**:
   Include this CSS in your global stylesheet so collaborative cursors and selection flags render cleanly:
   ```css
   .collaboration-cursor__caret {
     border-left: 1px solid #0d0d0d;
     border-right: 1px solid #0d0d0d;
     margin-left: -1px;
     margin-right: -1px;
     pointer-events: none;
     position: relative;
     word-break: normal;
   }

   .collaboration-cursor__label {
     border-radius: 3px 3px 3px 0;
     color: #fff;
     font-size: 11px;
     font-weight: 600;
     left: -1px;
     line-height: normal;
     padding: 1px 4px;
     position: absolute;
     top: -1.4em;
     user-select: none;
     white-space: nowrap;
   }
   ```

### 6. Recommended Next Features to Build
1. **Slash Commands (`/`)**: Use TipTap’s `Suggestion` plugin to trigger headings, bullet points, callouts, and code blocks.
2. **Version History**: Store snapshots of the document state using `Y.encodeStateAsUpdate(ydoc)` whenever an idle threshold is crossed (e.g., 5 minutes after typing ceases).
3. **Full-Text Search**: Implement vector search using Supabase `pgvector` or Algolia to enable semantic query parsing across all team notes.

Here's a fully working prototype in a single HTML file. It includes the core features a team notes app needs: shared notebooks, live-feeling collaboration, comments, tags, and search. Save it as `index.html` and open it in a browser.

```html
// ---------- Data & state ----------
const TEAM = [
  { id: 'you',  name: 'You (Sam)',   color: '#4f6cff', online: true  },
  { id: 'ana',  name: 'Ana Torres',  color: '#e0862a', online: true  },
  { id: 'raj',  name: 'Raj Patel',   color: '#8a4fcc', online: true  },
  { id: 'lee',  name: 'Jordan Lee',  color: '#2fa06c', online: false },
];
const ALL_TAGS = ['meeting', 'idea', 'spec', 'todo', 'decision'];

let notes = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('teamnotes') || 'null') || seed();
let activeId = notes[0]?.id ?? null;
let activeTag = null;
let saveTimer;

function seed() {
  return [{
    id: crypto.randomUUID(), title: 'Sprint 14 Planning',
    body: '<p><b>Goals</b></p><ul><li>Ship shared comments v1</li><li>Fix sync latency</li></ul><p><i>Action items assigned in comments 👇</i></p>',
    tags: ['meeting'], author: 'ana', created: Date.now() - 864e5, updated: Date.now() - 36e5,
    comments: [
      { id: 1, author: 'raj', text: 'I can take the latency ticket.', time: Date.now() - 72e5 },
      { id: 2, author: 'ana', text: 'Nice. Let\'s demo on Thursday.', time: Date.now() - 36e5 },
    ],
  }];
}

const persist = () => localStorage.setItem('teamnotes', JSON.stringify(notes));

// ---------- Rendering ----------
const $ = id => document.getElementById(id);
const member = id => TEAM.find(m => m.id === id) || { name: id, color: '#999' };
const avatar = (m, extra='') => `<div class="avatar" style="background:${m.color}" title="${m.name}">${m.name.split(' ').map(w=>w[0]).join('').slice(0,2)}</div>`;

function renderTeam() {
  $('team-list').innerHTML = TEAM.map(m =>
    `<div class="member">${avatar(m)} ${m.name}<span class="dot ${m.online?'':'off'}"></span></div>`).join('');
}

function renderFilters() {
  $('tag-filters').innerHTML = ALL_TAGS.map(t =>
    `<button class="chip ${t===activeTag?'active':''}" onclick="setTag('${t}')">${t}</button>`).join('');
}
function setTag(t) { activeTag = activeTag === t ? null : t; renderFilters(); renderList(); }

function filtered() {
  const q = $('search').value.toLowerCase();
  return notes.filter(n =>
    (!activeTag || n.tags.includes(activeTag)) &&
    (!q || (n.title + n.body).toLowerCase().includes(q))
  ).sort((a,b) => b.updated - a.updated);
}

function renderList() {
  $('note-list').innerHTML = filtered().map(n => {
    const preview = n.body.replace(/<[^>]*>/g,' ').slice(0, 60);
    return `<div class="note-item ${n.id===activeId?'active':''}" onclick="openNote('${n.id}')">
      <h4>${esc(n.title) || 'Untitled'}</h4><p>${esc(preview)}</p>
      <div class="meta">${avatar(member(n.author))} ${timeAgo(n.updated)}
      ${n.tags.map(t=>`<span class="tag">${t}</span>`).join('')}
      ${n.comments.length ? `💬 ${n.comments.length}` : ''}</div></div>`;
  }).join('') || '<div style="padding:20px;color:var(--muted);font-size:13px">No notes found</div>';
}

function openNote(id) {
  activeId = id;
  const n = notes.find(x => x.id === id);
  $('empty-state').style.display = n ? 'none' : 'flex';
  $('editor-area').style.display = n ? 'flex' : 'none';
  if (!n) return renderList();
  $('title-input').value = n.title;
  $('editor').innerHTML = n.body;
  renderTags(n); renderComments(n); renderViewers(n); renderList();
}

function renderTags(n) {
  $('note-tags').innerHTML = ALL_TAGS.map(t =>
    `<button class="chip ${n.tags.includes(t)?'active':''}" onclick="toggleTag('${t}')">${t}</button>`).join('');
}
function toggleTag(t) {
  const n = active(); if (!n) return;
  n.tags = n.tags.includes(t) ? n.tags.filter(x=>x!==t) : [...n.tags, t];
  touch(n); renderTags(n); renderList();
}

function renderComments(n) {
  $('comments').innerHTML = n.comments.map(c => {
    const m = member(c.author);
    return `<div class="comment"><div class="top">${avatar(m)}<b>${m.name}</b><time>${timeAgo(c.time)}</time></div>
    <div class="body">${esc(c.text)}</div></div>`;
  }).join('') || '<div style="color:var(--muted);font-size:13px">No comments yet</div>';
  $('comments').scrollTop = $('comments').scrollHeight;
}

// Simulated presence: random teammates "viewing" the note
function renderViewers(n) {
  const online = TEAM.filter(m => m.online && Math.random() > .4);
  if (!online.find(m => m.id === 'you')) online.unshift(member('you'));
  $('viewers').innerHTML = online.map(avatar).join('');
}

// ---------- Editing ----------
const active = () => notes.find(n => n.id === activeId);
function touch(n) {
  n.updated = Date.now();
  clearTimeout(saveTimer);
  $('saved-status').textContent = 'Saving…';
  saveTimer = setTimeout(() => { persist(); $('saved-status').textContent = 'Saved ✓'; renderList(); }, 400);
}

$('editor').addEventListener('input', e => { const n = active(); if (n) { n.body = e.target.innerHTML; touch(n); } });
$('title-input').addEventListener('input', e => { const n = active(); if (n) { n.title = e.target.value; touch(n); } });

// Markdown-style shortcuts inside the editor
$('editor').addEventListener('keydown', e => {
  if (e.key === ' ') {
    const sel = getSelection(); const node = sel.anchorNode;
    if (!node?.nodeType === 3) return;
    const text = node.textContent;
    const map = { '# ': 'h3', '## ': 'h4', '- ': 'ul', '> ': 'blockquote', '/note ': 'p' };
    for (const [k, tag] of Object.entries(map)) {
      if (text.startsWith(k)) {
        e.preventDefault();
        const el = document.createElement(tag === 'ul' ? 'ul' : tag);
        if (tag === 'ul') { const li = document.createElement('li'); li.textContent = text.slice(2); el.appendChild(li); }
        else el.textContent = text.slice(k.length);
        node.parentNode.replaceChild(el, node);
        placeCaret(el, 0);
      }
    }
  }
});

$('new-btn').onclick = () => {
  const n = { id: crypto.randomUUID(), title: '', body: '', tags: [], author: 'you',
              created: Date.now(), updated: Date.now(), comments: [] };
  notes.unshift(n); persist(); openNote(n.id); $('title-input').focus();
};
$('delete-btn').onclick = () => {
  if (!active() || !confirm('Delete this note for everyone?')) return;
  notes = notes.filter(n => n.id !== activeId);
  persist(); activeId = notes[0]?.id ?? null; openNote(activeId);
};

function addComment() {
  const n = active(); const text = $('comment-input').value.trim();
  if (!n || !text) return;
  n.comments.push({ id: Date.now(), author: 'you', text, time: Date.now() });
  $('comment-input').value = '';
  touch(n); renderComments(n);
}
$('comment-btn').onclick = addComment;
$('comment-input').addEventListener('keydown', e => e.key === 'Enter' && addComment());

$('search').addEventListener('input', renderList);

// ---------- Utils ----------
function esc(s=''){ return s.replace(/</g,'&lt;'); }
function timeAgo(t){ const s=(Date.now()-t)/1000;
  return s<60?'just now':s<3600?Math.floor(s/60)+'m ago':s<86400?Math.floor(s/3600)+'h ago':Math.floor(s/86400)+'d ago'; }
function placeCaret(el){ const r=document.createRange(); r.selectNodeContents(el); r.collapse(false);
  const s=getSelection(); s.removeAllRanges(); s.addRange(r); }

// Simulated teammate activity so the app feels "shared"
setInterval(() => {
  const m = TEAM[1 + Math.floor(Math.random()*3)];
  m.online = Math.random() > .25;
  renderTeam();
}, 7000);

// ---------- Init ----------
renderTeam(); renderFilters(); renderList(); openNote(activeId);
</script>
</body>
</html>
```

Output

**Recommendation: use managed Postgres** (e.g., Neon, Supabase, RDS, Cloud SQL, Railway). For a 2-person SaaS with low traffic and uncertain requirements, Postgres is the safest default: relational when you need it, JSONB when you don’t, strong ecosystem, easy local dev, and low operational burden if managed.

## Quick comparison

| Dimension | Postgres | SQLite | DynamoDB |
|---|---|---|---|
| Data model | Relational + JSONB | Relational/embedded file | Key-value/document, single-table design |
| Schema evolution | Easy with migrations | Easy, but looser typing | Hard: access patterns must be known upfront |
| Scaling | Vertical + read replicas; shard later | Single writer; HA/multi-instance hard | Automatic horizontal scale |
| Ops burden | Low managed; medium self-hosted | Very low single-node; HA/backups manual | Serverless, but IAM/capacity/GSI/cost monitoring |
| Cost | Predictable, often $0–50/mo small | Near zero | Pay-per-request can spike; capacity planning |
| Local dev | Excellent | Excellent | DynamoDB Local/emulator, not identical |
| Best fit | Uncertain requirements, general SaaS | Single-instance/edge/offline apps | Known access patterns, massive scale |

## Main tradeoffs

**Postgres** gives you the most flexibility. You can model core entities relationally, use JSONB for evolving fields, add indexes, joins, transactions, full-text search, and extensions. It handles low traffic trivially and can scale much further than most small SaaS apps ever need. The main cost is that you must manage schema migrations, indexes, and connection pooling—but managed providers remove most server ops.

**SQLite** is the simplest and cheapest. For a single-server app or local-first/offline product, it’s excellent. The problem is future SaaS growth: one writer at a time, awkward multi-instance deployment, and backups/HA/replication become manual or depend on tools like Litestream/Turso. It’s a great MVP choice, but it can become a migration project once you need multiple app servers, serverless functions, or zero-downtime HA.

**DynamoDB** scales beautifully if you know your access patterns. But “uncertain future requirements” is exactly the wrong input for DynamoDB. You must design around queries, denormalize aggressively, avoid joins, and often use single-table design. That is high cognitive overhead for 2 developers before product-market fit. It’s also AWS-vendor-locked and can produce surprising costs if indexes/scan patterns are wrong.

## Migration risk

- **SQLite → Postgres:** Usually low-to-moderate. ORMs help, but watch SQL dialect differences, auto-increment/identity, booleans, dates, upserts, and concurrency semantics. This is a common, well-trodden path.
- **Postgres → DynamoDB:** High. You’d need to redesign around access patterns, denormalize, and likely rewrite query-heavy code. Do it only if scale demands it.
- **DynamoDB → Postgres:** High. Export/transform/backfill and reconcile are painful; you may need dual writes or CDC.
- **Postgres → SQLite:** Low-to-moderate for a single-instance app, but usually a downgrade for a growing SaaS.

Starting with Postgres keeps your migration options open. You can defer hard scaling decisions until you actually need them.

## Operational burden

- **Managed Postgres:** Provider handles backups, failover, patching, and monitoring basics. You handle migrations, indexes, query performance, and connection limits. For serverless, use a pooler like PgBouncer, Supabase pooler, or Neon.
- **SQLite:** Almost zero ops on one box. But backups, replication, multi-region, and concurrent writes become your problem as soon as you scale beyond one instance.
- **DynamoDB:** No servers to patch, but you must monitor capacity/on-demand spend, hot partitions, GSIs, streams, backups, IAM, and local-dev parity.

## What would change the recommendation

- **Choose SQLite/Turso** if the product becomes local-first, offline-first, single-tenant, edge-deployed, or you can run on one server for the foreseeable future and want zero DB ops.
- **Choose DynamoDB** if access patterns are known and stable, you need near-infinite horizontal scale, single-digit-millisecond latency at global scale, serverless with no connection pooling, and your team has deep DynamoDB experience.
- **Stay with Postgres** if you need relational queries, reporting, joins, transactions, or you expect requirements to keep changing.
- **Consider CockroachDB/Spanner** if you truly need global strong consistency and horizontal scale later.
- **Reconsider if costs spike:** if managed Postgres becomes too expensive at very low traffic, a single-node SQLite deployment may be better until growth justifies the move.

For this scenario, start on managed Postgres, use an ORM/migration tool, keep schema portable, and don’t over-engineer. It maximizes iteration speed now while preserving a reasonable path for most future SaaS requirements.

Output

### Recommendation: **PostgreSQL**

For a 2-developer team with low initial traffic and **uncertain future requirements**, **PostgreSQL is the strongest choice.** 

The key constraint here is *uncertain requirements*. DynamoDB requires you to model data around known access patterns upfront, which is fatal for early iteration. SQLite is operationally simpler, but handling multiple app instances, backups, and concurrent writes introduces subtle architecture hurdles you don't need right now. Managed PostgreSQL provides near-zero operational overhead, unmatched flexibility (relational + JSONB), and the lowest overall risk.

---

### Comparison Breakdown

| Criteria | PostgreSQL | SQLite | DynamoDB |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Data Modeling Flexibility** | **Very High**: Relations, JSONB, Full-text, Enums | **High**: Relational, dynamically typed | **Very Low**: Requires knowing query patterns in advance |
| **Operational Burden** | **Low** (via managed services) | **Near-zero** (embedded) to **Medium** (if distributed) | **Zero** (fully serverless) |
| **Early Dev Velocity** | **High**: Rich ORM ecosystem, easy migrations | **High**: Zero setup, runs in-process | **Low**: Complex single-table design & strict querying |
| **Scaling (Write Concurrency)** | **High**: Row-level locking, robust connections | **Low/Medium**: Single-writer limitation | **Practically Infinite**: Auto-partitioned |
| **Ecosystem & Tooling** | **Best-in-class**: Admin UIs, libraries, extensions | **Good**: Universal language support, limited enterprise tooling | **Moderate**: AWS-centric tools, non-standard query API |
| **Initial Cost** | Free – $25/mo (managed) | $0 (runs in app process) | Free tier / pennies (pay-per-request) |

---

### Tradeoffs Analysis

#### 1. PostgreSQL
* **The Good:** It handles ambiguity better than any database. Need strict relations? Use standard tables. Need unstructured document storage? Use `JSONB`. Need basic search? It has built-in full-text search. It allows you to build the app without committing prematurely to a specific paradigm.
* **The Tradeoff:** Costs slightly more than SQLite or DynamoDB at rock-bottom traffic (though serverless tiers like Neon or Supabase offer generous free tiers). It requires a live network connection and connection pool management (e.g., PgBouncer) if using serverless compute.

#### 2. SQLite
* **The Good:** Zero infrastructure. It lives inside your application process as a single file, eliminating network latency and cloud infrastructure bills. Great for local integration testing.
* **The Tradeoff:** Deployment friction. The moment you deploy your web app across two stateless containers (e.g., AWS ECS, Render, Fly.io, Heroku) behind a load balancer, standard SQLite breaks because writes can't be coordinated across separate ephemeral disks. Solutions exist (Litestream, LiteFS, Turso), but they introduce custom operational patterns.

#### 3. DynamoDB
* **The Good:** Zero server maintenance, instant provisioning, practically infinite scaling, and pay-per-request pricing that costs fractions of a cent at low traffic.
* **The Tradeoff:** **It is antithetical to "uncertain requirements."** To query DynamoDB efficiently without paying for full-table scans, you must know your read patterns before designing partition keys and Global Secondary Indexes (GSIs). Changing your data access patterns frequently in early SaaS development creates severe engineering friction.

---

### Operational Burden (for a 2-person team)

* **DynamoDB (Lowest infrastructure, highest design burden):** No patches, no instances, no connection pools. However, schema redesigns and GSI updates consume massive engineering hours.
* **PostgreSQL (Low infrastructure with managed services):** Using modern managed providers (Neon, Supabase, AWS RDS, Render Postgres) abstracts away hardware maintenance, backups, and replication. Your primary maintenance will be running standard database migrations (`ALTER TABLE`).
* **SQLite (Zero infra, but high deployment constraints):** Zero database ops, but your CI/CD and deployment pipeline must accommodate persistent storage volumes, single-node application hosting, or specialized replication tools (Litestream/LiteFS) for off-site backups and disaster recovery.

---

### Migration Risk

* **DynamoDB has the highest migration risk.** Migrating away from DynamoDB requires a complete redesign of your persistence layer. Code using the AWS SDK or specialized DynamoDB ORMs must be completely rewritten into relational queries.
* **PostgreSQL has the lowest migration risk.** It is the industry-standard lingua franca of backend development. Migrating from Postgres to another relational engine (or migrating from one Postgres cloud provider to another) is trivial via standard dump-and-restore tools (`pg_dump`).
* **SQLite has low migration risk.** SQLite schemas map easily to Postgres (many frameworks like Django/Rails let you develop on SQLite and ship on Postgres).

---

### What Would Change the Recommendation?

1. **Switch to DynamoDB if:**
   * Your data model is strictly key-value (e.g., session storage, API token lookups, user settings with no joining requirements).
   * Your team is building entirely within AWS serverless primitives (API Gateway + AWS Lambda) and will never need ad-hoc reporting or analytics.

2. **Switch to SQLite if:**
   * You are deploying your entire app as a **monolith on a single, persistent virtual machine** (e.g., a single Hetzner or DigitalOcean box) and do not plan to scale horizontally via containers in the near future.
   * You use modern edge-distributed SQLite platforms like **Turso** specifically because your app requires ultra-low latency reads distributed globally.

Output

## Recommendation: Managed Postgres

For a 2-person SaaS team with low traffic and unclear requirements, managed Postgres (Neon, Supabase, RDS, Cloud SQL) is the clear default. At low traffic, all three options handle the load fine — so the decision should hinge on flexibility and team fit, not performance.

## Quick comparison

| | Postgres | SQLite | DynamoDB |
|---|---|---|---|
| Query flexibility | Excellent (SQL, joins, ad-hoc reporting) | Good (SQL, but limited concurrency) | Poor (must design access patterns up front) |
| Handles uncertain requirements | Strong — JSONB + ad-hoc SQL | Moderate | Weak — new access patterns require re-modeling |
| Ops burden (managed) | Low | Lowest, but you own backups/replication | Lowest infra, highest design burden |
| Scale ceiling | Far beyond your likely need | Single node, one writer | Effectively unlimited |
| Ecosystem for a small team | Largest | Small | AWS-specific |

## Main tradeoffs

**Postgres**: Relational integrity, joins, transactions, and reporting matter for almost every SaaS (users, orgs, subscriptions, audit logs, admin dashboards). JSONB covers schema-flexible needs. The main cost is modest: a managed instance (~$15–50/mo at your scale) and connection pooling awareness as you grow.

**SQLite**: Zero-ops and genuinely fast for single-node use. But it has one writer at a time, no built-in network access, and a DIY durability story (Litestream helps). It's great for a single-server monolith, local-first apps, or edge deployments — less great for a multi-tenant SaaS that will grow write concurrency.

**DynamoDB**: The dealbreaker for your situation is that DynamoDB's model *requires knowing your access patterns in advance* — the exact opposite of "uncertain requirements." Adding a new query later often means building Streams/Lambda backfills for new indexes. No joins, no ad-hoc queries, painful reporting. Small teams using DynamoDB for generic SaaS data very commonly end up adding Postgres anyway, now with two databases to operate.

## Migration risk

- **SQLite → Postgres**: Real but manageable. SQL dialect, type system, and concurrency differences mean it's a project, not a copy. If you start on SQLite, use an ORM abstraction and avoid SQLite-specific tricks; migrate early rather than late.
- **Postgres → anything**: Rarely needed. Growth usually means additive changes — read replicas, caching, partitioning — not a rewrite.
- **DynamoDB → Postgres**: High cost. Data re-modeling plus dual-running systems. And ironically, this is the migration small teams most often end up needing.

The general principle: under uncertainty, choose the database that preserves optionality. Postgres data is portable and the SQL skills/tools are universal.

## Operational burden

- **SQLite**: Lowest in theory, but you own backups, replication, and monitoring — "no ops" can quietly become "no safety net."
- **Managed Postgres**: Provider handles patching, backups, failover. You own schema migrations (routine with tooling like Flyway/Alembic/Prisma) and query performance. This is the right burden for a 2-person team.
- **DynamoDB**: Provider owns everything, but you own data modeling, capacity mode decisions, and hot-partition monitoring. Ops shifts from database administration to application complexity.

## What would change my recommendation

**Switch to SQLite if**: you're deploying a single monolith on one box and value maximal simplicity, the app is read-heavy with low write concurrency, or you're doing local-first/edge (e.g., Cloudflare Workers, Turso). Accept that migration to Postgres later is a known cost.

**Switch to DynamoDB if**: you're building a component with *known, few, high-scale* access patterns — session storage, telemetry ingestion, high-write key-value data — or your team is deeply AWS-serverless-native with existing DynamoDB experience. Even then, it's often better as a *supplement* to Postgres for that one workload, not a replacement.

**Stay on Postgres and add later if**: requirements turn heavily analytical (add a warehouse or OLAP store), or a specific feature needs extreme write scale (add DynamoDB or a queue-backed store for just that piece). Postgres is the easiest base to extend from, which is exactly what an uncertain future calls for.

# Database Recommendation for Your Situation

## The Short Answer

**PostgreSQL, managed (Neon, Supabase, or RDS), is what I'd pick.** But I want to be honest about why, because the "correct" answer is less obvious than it looks, and SQLite is genuinely not the joke answer it's often treated as.

---

## How Each One Actually Behaves in Your Context

### SQLite

A single file. No server, no connection string, no `pg_hba.conf` nightmares. You write your ORM queries and they just work. For a SaaS with 2 devs and low traffic, this is not a toy — Hacker News ran on SQLite for years, and modern versions handle thousands of concurrent reads and hundreds of writes per second on a laptop.

**Where it bites you:** The moment you need two app instances sharing writes (which most SaaS deployment models eventually require), you're either stuck on a single server or duct-taping things with Litestream or rqlite. "Uncertain future requirements" is exactly the situation where you don't want to be thinking about write-concurrency topology. You also lose the rich relational features (foreign key enforcement across the full surface, `LATERAL` joins, CTEs in older builds, row-level security) that you'll eventually want once your data model grows beyond "users, projects, memberships."

### PostgreSQL

The default gravitational center of SaaS data. Every ORM, every migration tool, every SaaS boilerplate assumes Postgres. You get strong typing, JSONB for the "we don't know yet" parts of your schema, row-level security for multi-tenancy, and a migration path from 10 users to 10 million without a rearchitecture. The ecosystem (pgvector, pg_cron, pg_partman) means you rarely need a second database.

**The cost:** You are now an operator. Even with managed Postgres, you own the connection pool, the backup/restore drill, the migration review, the "why did the `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` lock my writes" meeting. For 2 developers, that's a real tax. And "managed" can mean 4–8x the cost of the raw compute, especially on AWS RDS.

### DynamoDB

Serverless, single-digit-millisecond latency at any scale, zero patching. Tempting on paper.

**Why it's wrong for you specifically:** Your SaaS almost certainly has relational data (users belong to orgs, orgs have subscriptions, memberships link them). You'll be writing `TransactWrite` calls to simulate a `JOIN`, building denormalized GSI copies of the same logical row, and doing application-level referential integrity. "Uncertain future requirements" is the death sentence for a NoSQL schema you can't `ALTER TABLE` — every query pattern you hadn't anticipated becomes a new index or a scan. And the pricing model (per-request read/write units) punishes the exploratory, chatty query patterns you'll write in year one before you've found your access patterns. You also lock yourself into AWS in a way that makes future migration genuinely painful.

---

## Side-by-Side on Your Actual Concerns

| | **SQLite** | **PostgreSQL (managed)** | **DynamoDB** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **2-dev operational burden** | Near zero. No server. No DBA. | Low-ish, but real. You still think about backups, connection pools, migration ordering, query plans. | Zero patching, but you architect around its constraints every sprint. That's its own burden. |
| **Iteration speed on uncertain schema** | Fast to start, increasingly awkward past ~15–20 tables. | Fastest overall. `ALTER TABLE`, JSONB columns, partial indexes — you iterate without pain. | Slowest. Schema is your access pattern. New query = new index = new data migration. |
| **Multi-tenancy support** | Row-level isolation is DIY. No RLS. | Built-in RLS, schema-per-tenant, partitioning. You'll thank yourself later. | You build it yourself out of partition keys and filters. |
| **Migration risk (leaving later)** | Low. SQLite→Postgres is a well-trodden dump-and-restore. | Low. Postgres→anything is the best-documented path in databases. | High. NoSQL→relational is a project, not a migration. You rewrite your data access layer. |
| **Scaling ceiling** | ~1 write-throughput bottleneck. Fine until you need >1 app instance writing. | Virtually unlimited with read replicas, partitioning, sharding later. | Virtually unlimited, but you pay per-request at every scale. |
| **Cost at 100 users** | ~$0. | ~$20–40/mo (Neon/Supabase) or ~$80–150 (RDS minimum). | ~$1–5/mo, deceptively cheap until it isn't. |
| **Cost at 100K users** | You've already moved to something else. | ~$200–500/mo for a beefy managed instance. | Can hit $1–5K+/mo easily on a chatty read pattern. Often the surprise. |

---

## The Recommendation and Why

**Managed PostgreSQL.** Start with Neon or Supabase (generous free tiers, branch-based dev/prod workflows, built-in connection pooling via PgBouncer so your 2 devs aren't managing a pooler). Graduate to RDS or a self-hosted instance only if you hit a specific wall.

The reason is not that Postgres is the "best" database — it isn't for every job. It's that **Postgres is the database you will least regret at month 18**, when your "uncertain future requirements" have crystallized into multi-tenancy, background jobs, analytics queries, a search feature, and a vector similarity lookup for a new AI feature. Every one of those is a `CREATE EXTENSION` or a query, not an architecture change. SQLite forces you to rewrite the data layer at the awkward middle moment. DynamoDB forces you to fight the data model every single sprint.

For 2 developers specifically, the single most valuable property is: **the database you choose should not be a second product you're building.** Postgres on a managed platform is closest to "it just stores my relational data the way I think about it."

---

## What Would Change This Recommendation

I'd flip to **SQLite** if:

- You're truly a solo or 2-person team, your data fits in one process, and you can commit to a **single app instance** (or you're happy with an in-process server like Fly.io with a volume) for the next 2–3 years. The operational simplicity is genuinely worth the scaling ceiling. Start SQLite, extract to Postgres when you need a second instance. The migration is a `sqlite3 .dump` and a `psql` restore. It's a weekend.
- You're building something where the "database" is mostly a key-value cache or a config store, not relational data.

I'd flip to **DynamoDB** if:

- Your data is genuinely document-shaped and query patterns are *known and stable* (not "uncertain"). Think: a clickstream analytics store, a game leaderboard, a per-request session store.
- You are already 100% committed to AWS and your team has NoSQL experience.
- You're building a data platform where write throughput at scale is the primary requirement and relational joins are rare.

I'd flip away from Postgres entirely (toward something like CockroachDB or a sharded setup) only if:

- You have a regulatory/data-residency requirement for multiple regions with strong consistency. That's a problem you won't have at your stage, and if you did, this question would look very different.

---

## One Practical Note

Whatever you pick, **do schema migrations from day one**. The single most common failure mode for 2-person SaaS teams is "we just ran `ALTER TABLE` on production." A migration tool (Prisma Migrate, Flyway, dbmate, or whatever your ORM integrates with) costs you 30 minutes of setup and saves you the 2 a.m. conversation where the deploy broke and nobody can roll back the schema. This matters more than the database choice.

   Example:
   ```ts

   ```
   returns `1799.1`, not an integer cent value. Since the function is named `totalCents` and previously always returned integer cents, this is a correctness/API regression. Pick and document a rounding policy, e.g. round the discount amount or the final total, and keep the result an integer.

2. **Coupon values are not validated.**
   - `couponPercent > 100` produces a negative total.

   It treats `0` and `undefined` as “no coupon”, which is okay, but it also silently ignores `NaN` and `null` at runtime. Prefer an explicit check after validation, such as `couponPercent === undefined || couponPercent === 0`.

   - no coupon / `undefined`
   - `0%`

   - `100%`
   - rounding-sensitive values, e.g. `1999` cents at `10%`
   - invalid values: negative, `>100`, `NaN`, `Infinity`

   - multiple items with different quantities

   `couponPercent` does not say whether it is a discount or tax, and as an optional positional parameter it becomes awkward if more adjustments are added later. Consider an options object if this area will grow. At minimum, document that `10` means “10% off”.

A safer implementation direction would validate the coupon, compute an integer discount or total with an explicit rounding rule, and return integer cents consistently.

Here is my review of the changes.

### Findings (Ordered by Severity)

#### 1. [High] Floating-Point Cents (Fractional Currency)
* **Issue:** `subtotal - subtotal * couponPercent / 100` will frequently return a floating-point number.
* **Impact:** `totalCents` is expected to return an integer. Returning fractional cents (e.g., `849.15`) can cause failures or unexpected behavior downstream—especially with payment processors (like Stripe) or database schemas expecting integer cents.
* **Fix:** Explicitly round the result (typically `Math.round`, depending on your accounting rules).

#### 2. [High] Unvalidated `couponPercent` Bounds
* **Issue:** There is no validation on the range of `couponPercent`.
* **Impact:** 
  * If `couponPercent > 100`, the function returns a negative total (store pays customer).
  * If `couponPercent < 0`, it acts as a surcharge instead of a discount.
  * If `couponPercent` is `NaN`, it will propagate through arithmetic.
* **Fix:** Validate that `couponPercent` falls between `0` and `100` (throw an error or clamp the value based on business requirements).

#### 3. [Medium] Missing Unit Tests
* **Issue:** No tests were included in this PR.
* **Impact:** Regression risk and unverified edge cases.
* **Required test cases:**
  * Fractional cent rounding (e.g., $9.99 with a 15% discount).
  * Boundary values: `couponPercent = 0` and `couponPercent = 100`.
  * Fractional percentages (e.g., `12.5%`).
  * Empty cart (`items = []`) with a coupon applied.
  * Invalid values (negative, `> 100`, `NaN`) if validation is added.
  * Backward compatibility (calling without the second argument).

#### 4. [Low] Falsy Check on Numeric Argument
* **Issue:** `if (!couponPercent)` relies on JavaScript falsiness.
* **Impact:** While `0` correctly short-circuits to `subtotal`, relying on truthiness for numbers can mask subtle bugs (e.g., silently accepting `NaN` or unexpected inputs).
* **Fix:** Use an explicit check like `if (couponPercent === undefined)`.

---

### Suggested Implementation

```typescript
export function totalCents(items: CartItem[], couponPercent?: number): number {
  const subtotal = items.reduce((sum, item) => sum + item.priceCents * item.quantity, 0);

  if (couponPercent === undefined || couponPercent === 0) {
    return subtotal;
  }

  if (couponPercent < 0 || couponPercent > 100 || Number.isNaN(couponPercent)) {
    throw new RangeError("couponPercent must be between 0 and 100");
  }

  const discountCents = Math.round((subtotal * couponPercent) / 100);
  return subtotal - discountCents;
}
```

*(Note: Calculating `discountCents` and subtracting ensures the discount itself is also an integer amount, which helps if you ever need to display the discount line-item).*

## Review: coupon support in `totalCents`

Right direction, small diff, backward-compatible signature. Two correctness bugs need fixing before merge.

### High

**1. Breaks the integer-cents invariant (floating point + fractional cents).**
`subtotal * couponPercent / 100` routinely produces non-integer or float-noise results:

- subtotal `333`, coupon `10` → `299.7`
- subtotal `999`, coupon `3` → `969.0300000000001`

Everything downstream (rendering, persistence, payment gateway, comparisons) assumes `totalCents` returns an integer number of cents. Round explicitly and pick a policy (see #3):

```ts
const discount = Math.round((subtotal * couponPercent) / 100);
return subtotal - discount;
```

**2. No bounds validation on `couponPercent`.**
- Negative value → the "discount" is a surcharge (customer pays *more*).
- `> 100` → negative total.
- `Infinity` → `-Infinity`.

If this value ever comes from user input, that's exploitable. Validate and throw:

```ts
if (!Number.isInteger(couponPercent) || couponPercent < 0 || couponPercent > 100) {
  throw new RangeError(`couponPercent must be an integer in [0, 100]; got ${couponPercent}`);
}
```

(If fractional coupons like `12.5` are a real requirement, drop the `isInteger` check but keep the range check and define rounding. If not, enforcing integers makes the rounding question disappear.)

### Medium

**3. Rounding policy is undefined.**
For subtotal `999` / coupon `10`, is the customer charged `899` or `899.1 → 899`? Right now it's "whatever the float does." This affects revenue and sometimes tax/compliance. Decide, document it in a JSDoc comment on the function, and lock it in with tests. Also flag to whoever owns pricing: cart-level discounts may not reconcile with per-item discounts needed for partial refunds — worth confirming that's out of scope rather than unknown.

**4. No tests in the diff.**
Required cases:
- No coupon / `undefined` / `0` → subtotal (regression on existing behavior)
- `100` → `0`
- Fractional result (e.g. `999` @ `10%`) → exact expected integer per the chosen rounding rule
- `-1` and `101` → throws
- Empty cart with a coupon → `0`
- Sweep/property test asserting `Number.isInteger(result)` across many subtotal/percent pairs

### Nits

- `if (!couponPercent)` conflates `undefined`, `0`, and `NaN`. It happens to behave acceptably here, but prefer a default parameter (`couponPercent = 0`) plus the explicit validation above — the falsy check becomes unnecessary.
- Consider a small `discountCents(subtotal, percent)` helper so callers/tests can verify the discount and total agree, and so later promo stacking has a place to live. Not blocking.
- One-line JSDoc stating the invariant: returns integer cents.

### Non-issue confirmed

Optional param means existing callers are unaffected — no migration needed. Just make sure any caller doing exact equality checks against the return value gets the test coverage above.

## Code Review: `totalCents` coupon support

### 1. 🔴 Floating-point result breaks the "cents" contract

The function is named `totalCents` and the type system implies integer cents throughout the codebase (`priceCents: number`). But `subtotal * couponPercent / 100` produces fractional results:

```ts
totalCents([{ sku: "A", priceCents: 999, quantity: 1 }], 33)
// → 999 - 999 * 33 / 100 = 999 - 329.67 = 669.33  ← not a cent value
```

Downstream consumers (tax engines, payment gateways, `toFixed(2)` display logic) will silently receive sub-cent precision or, worse, accumulate rounding drift across multiple coupons/items.

**Fix:** round to an integer and document the rounding rule:

```ts
return Math.round(subtotal - (subtotal * couponPercent) / 100);
```

---

### 2. 🟠 `!couponPercent` conflates `0` with "no coupon"

`if (!couponPercent)` treats `0`, `undefined`, and `null` identically. A 0 % coupon is a legitimate (if unusual) input that should short-circuit *after* validation, not be silently swallowed. More practically, if a future refactor makes the body `if (couponPercent) { … } else { return subtotal; }` the logic is identical—but a reviewer can't tell whether that's intentional or a bug.

**Fix:** make the intent explicit:

```ts
if (couponPercent == null) return subtotal;  // catches undefined & null, but NOT 0
```

---

### 3. 🟠 No bounds validation on `couponPercent`

- A negative value **increases** the total (`subtotal - subtotal * -20 / 100`).
- A value > 100 produces a **negative** order total.
- `NaN` passes the `!` guard check only by accident (`!NaN === true`), but a string-cast number from a CSV import (`"50"` as `any`) would slip through `!` and produce `NaN` downstream.

At minimum, add a runtime guard or narrow the type:

```ts
export function totalCents(items: CartItem[], couponPercent?: number): number {
  // …
  if (couponPercent != null) {
    if (!Number.isFinite(couponPercent) || couponPercent < 0 || couponPercent > 100) {
      throw new RangeError(`couponPercent must be 0–100, got ${couponPercent}`);
    }
    return Math.round(subtotal - (subtotal * couponPercent) / 100);
  }
  return subtotal;
}
```

---

### 4. 🟡 No tests in the diff

This is a money-touching function. At minimum, add tests for:

| Case | Expected |
|---|---|
| No coupon, empty cart | `0` |
| No coupon, normal cart | existing behavior (regression guard) |
| 0 % coupon | same as no coupon, **integer** |
| 33 % on a subtotal that isn't evenly divisible | rounded integer |
| 100 % coupon | `0` |
| `undefined` / omitted | `subtotal` |
| Negative / > 100 / NaN | throws `RangeError` |

---

### 5. 🟡 Minor / style notes

- **Return-type annotation.** Add `: number` (or `: integer` via a branded type if you have one) so the signature is self-documenting now that the body can produce floats.
- **Backward-compat check.** The optional param is additive, so existing call sites compile unchanged—good. But confirm no caller passes `couponPercent` positionally through a spread like `totalCents(...args)` where `args` might carry an unexpected third element.
- **Consider a separate function or options bag** if the discount logic grows (stackable coupons, per-item vs. order-level). A `totalCents(items, { couponPercent })` shape is easier to extend than positional `?` params.

---

**Verdict:** request changes on points **1–3** before merge. The rest are advisory.

```html
    gap: 14px;
  }

  .sr-only {
    position: absolute;
    width: 1px; height: 1px;
    padding: 0; margin: -1px;
    overflow: hidden;
    clip-path: inset(50%);
    white-space: nowrap;
    border: 0;
  }

  /* ===================== Top bar ===================== */
  .topbar {
    display: flex;
    align-items: center;
    justify-content: space-between;
    gap: 12px;
    padding-inline: 4px;
  }

  .topbar h1 {
    margin: 0;
    font-size: 0.82rem;
    font-weight: 700;
    letter-spacing: 0.2em;
    text-transform: uppercase;
    color: var(--muted);
  }

  .ghost-btn {
    font: inherit;
    font-size: 0.82rem;
    font-weight: 600;
    color: var(--muted);
    background: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.04);
    border: 1px solid var(--border);
    border-radius: 999px;
    padding: 8px 14px;
    cursor: pointer;
    transition: background-color 0.2s ease, color 0.2s ease, border-color 0.2s ease;
  }

  .ghost-btn:hover { background: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.09); color: var(--text); }

  /* ===================== Card ===================== */
  .card {
    display: flex;
    flex-direction: column;
    align-items: center;
    gap: 20px;
    padding: clamp(20px, 5vw, 30px);
    background: linear-gradient(180deg, var(--surface), var(--surface-2));
    border: 1px solid var(--border);
    border-radius: 26px;
    box-shadow:
      0 26px 60px -20px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.75),
      inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.045);
  }

  .mode {
    margin: 0;
    font-size: 0.75rem;
    font-weight: 700;
    letter-spacing: 0.2em;
    text-transform: uppercase;
    color: var(--accent);
    transition: color 0.35s ease;
  }

  /* ===================== Dial ===================== */
  .dial {
    position: relative;
    width: min(72vw, 280px);
    aspect-ratio: 1 / 1;
    display: grid;
    place-items: center;
  }

  .dial svg {
    width: 100%;
    height: 100%;
    transform: rotate(-90deg); /* start the ring at 12 o'clock */
  }

  .track {
    fill: none;
    stroke: var(--track);
    stroke-width: 10;
  }

  .progress {
    fill: none;
    stroke: var(--accent);
    stroke-width: 10;
    stroke-linecap: round;
    transition: stroke-dashoffset 0.25s linear, stroke 0.35s ease;
  }

  .dial.pulse { animation: dial-pulse 0.7s ease-out 2; }

  @keyframes dial-pulse {
    0%, 100% { transform: scale(1); }
    45%      { transform: scale(1.045); }
  }

  .readout {
    position: absolute;
    inset: 0;
    display: flex;
    flex-direction: column;
    align-items: center;
    justify-content: center;
    gap: 6px;
    pointer-events: none;
  }

  .time {
    font-size: clamp(2.5rem, 14vw, 3.4rem);
    font-weight: 700;
    line-height: 1;
    letter-spacing: -0.02em;
    font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
    font-feature-settings: "tnum";
  }

  .sub {
    font-size: 0.78rem;
    color: var(--muted);
    font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
  }

  /* ===================== Controls ===================== */
  .controls {
    display: flex;
    gap: 10px;
    width: 100%;
  }

  .btn {
    font: inherit;
    font-weight: 650;
    border-radius: 999px;
    border: 1px solid transparent;
    padding: 14px 18px;
    cursor: pointer;
    transition: transform 0.12s ease, background-color 0.2s ease,
                border-color 0.2s ease, color 0.2s ease, filter 0.2s ease;
  }

  .btn:active { transform: scale(0.975); }

  .btn:focus-visible {
    outline: 2px solid var(--accent);
    outline-offset: 3px;
  }

  .btn.primary {
    flex: 1 1 auto;
    background: var(--accent);
    color: var(--accent-ink);
    box-shadow: 0 12px 26px -12px var(--accent);
  }

  .btn.primary:hover { filter: brightness(1.08); }

  .btn.ghost {
    flex: 0 0 auto;
    background: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.05);
    border-color: var(--border);
    color: var(--text);
  }

  .btn.ghost:hover { background: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.1); }

  /* ===================== Session dots ===================== */
  .dots {
    display: flex;
    gap: 8px;
    flex-wrap: wrap;
    justify-content: center;
    min-height: 10px;
  }

  .dot {
    width: 9px;
    height: 9px;
    border-radius: 50%;
    background: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.14);
    transition: background-color 0.3s ease, transform 0.3s ease, box-shadow 0.3s ease;
  }

  .dot.filled {
    background: var(--accent);
    box-shadow: 0 0 10px var(--accent);
    transform: scale(1.2);
  }

  /* ===================== Settings ===================== */
  .settings {
    display: grid;
    grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(135px, 1fr));
    gap: 14px;
    padding: 18px;
    background: var(--surface);
    border: 1px solid var(--border);
    border-radius: 20px;
    animation: panel-in 0.22s ease;
  }

  .settings[hidden] { display: none; }

  @keyframes panel-in {
    from { opacity: 0; transform: translateY(-6px); }
    to   { opacity: 1; transform: translateY(0); }
  }

  .field {
    display: flex;
    flex-direction: column;
    gap: 6px;
    min-width: 0;
  }

  .field label {
    font-size: 0.7rem;
    font-weight: 700;
    letter-spacing: 0.08em;
    text-transform: uppercase;
    color: var(--muted);
  }

  .field input[type="number"] {
    font: inherit;
    font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
    color: var(--text);
    background: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.045);
    border: 1px solid var(--border);
    border-radius: 12px;
    padding: 10px 12px;
    width: 100%;
    transition: border-color 0.2s ease, background-color 0.2s ease;
  }

  .field input[type="number"]:focus {
    outline: none;
    border-color: var(--accent);
    background: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.07);
  }

  .check {
    grid-column: 1 / -1;
    display: flex;
    align-items: center;
    gap: 10px;
    font-size: 0.9rem;
    color: var(--text);
    cursor: pointer;
    padding-top: 2px;
  }

  .check input {
    width: 18px;
    height: 18px;
    accent-color: var(--accent);
    cursor: pointer;
  }

  .hint {
    margin: 0;
    text-align: center;
    font-size: 0.74rem;
    color: var(--muted);
    letter-spacing: 0.02em;
  }

  /* ===================== Toast + flash alert ===================== */
  .toast {
    position: fixed;
    left: 50%;
    bottom: clamp(16px, 4vh, 34px);
    transform: translate(-50%, 18px);
    max-width: min(90vw, 380px);
    padding: 12px 18px;
    font-size: 0.88rem;
    text-align: center;
    color: var(--text);
    background: var(--surface-2);
    border: 1px solid var(--border);
    border-left: 3px solid var(--accent);
    border-radius: 14px;
    box-shadow: 0 20px 44px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.6);
    opacity: 0;
    pointer-events: none;
    z-index: 60;
    transition: opacity 0.28s ease, transform 0.28s ease;
  }

  .toast.show {
    opacity: 1;
    transform: translate(-50%, 0);
  }

  .flash {
    position: fixed;
    inset: 0;
    z-index: 80;
    background: var(--accent);
    opacity: 0;
    pointer-events: none;
  }

  .flash.on { animation: flash-pulse 1.1s ease-out 2; }

  @keyframes flash-pulse {
    0%   { opacity: 0; }
    10%  { opacity: 0.42; }
    100% { opacity: 0; }
  }

  /* ===================== Motion preferences ===================== */
  @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
    *,
    *::before,
    *::after {
      animation-duration: 0.001ms !important;
      animation-iteration-count: 1 !important;
      transition-duration: 0.001ms !important;
    }
  }
</style>
</head>
<body data-mode="work">

  <!-- Full-screen colour pulse used as the "time is up" alert -->
  <div class="flash" id="flash" aria-hidden="true"></div>

  <main class="app">
    <header class="topbar">
      <h1>Pomodoro</h1>
      <button type="button" class="ghost-btn" id="settingsToggle"
              aria-expanded="false" aria-controls="settings">Settings</button>
    </header>

    <section class="card" aria-labelledby="modeLabel">
      <p class="mode" id="modeLabel">Focus</p>

      <div class="dial" id="dial">
        <svg viewBox="0 0 200 200" aria-hidden="true" focusable="false">
          <circle class="track" cx="100" cy="100" r="90"></circle>
          <circle class="progress" id="ring" cx="100" cy="100" r="90"></circle>
        </svg>
        <div class="readout">
          <div class="time" id="time">25:00</div>
          <div class="sub" id="sub">Session 1 of 4</div>
        </div>
      </div>

      <div class="controls">
        <button type="button" class="btn primary" id="primary">Start</button>
        <button type="button" class="btn ghost" id="reset" title="Restart the current interval">Reset</button>
        <button type="button" class="btn ghost" id="skip" title="Jump to the next interval">Skip</button>
      </div>

      <div class="dots" id="dots" aria-hidden="true"></div>
    </section>

    <form class="settings" id="settings" hidden novalidate>
      <div class="field">
        <label for="set-work">Focus (min)</label>
        <input id="set-work" type="number" min="1" max="180" step="1"
               inputmode="numeric" autocomplete="off">
      </div>
      <div class="field">
        <label for="set-short">Short break (min)</label>
        <input id="set-short" type="number" min="1" max="60" step="1"
               inputmode="numeric" autocomplete="off">
      </div>
      <div class="field">
        <label for="set-long">Long break (min)</label>
        <input id="set-long" type="number" min="1" max="120" step="1"
               inputmode="numeric" autocomplete="off">
      </div>
      <div class="field">
        <label for="set-every">Long break every</label>
        <input id="set-every" type="number" min="1" max="12" step="1"
               inputmode="numeric" autocomplete="off">
      </div>
      <label class="check" for="set-auto">
        <input id="set-auto" type="checkbox">
        <span>Auto-start the next interval</span>
      </label>
    </form>

    <p class="hint">Space = start/pause · R = reset · S = skip</p>

    <!-- Screen-reader announcements (visual users get the toast + colour pulse) -->
    <p class="sr-only" id="status" role="status" aria-live="polite"></p>
  </main>

  <div class="toast" id="toast" aria-hidden="true"></div>

<script>
(function () {
  'use strict';

  /* =========================================================
     Constants
     ========================================================= */
  const RING_RADIUS = 90;
  const RING_CIRCUMFERENCE = 2 * Math.PI * RING_RADIUS;

  const MODE_LABEL = { work: 'Focus', short: 'Short break', long: 'Long break' };

  const LIMITS = {
    work: [1, 180],
    short: [1, 60],
    long: [1, 120],
    longEvery: [1, 12]
  };

  const DEFAULTS = { work: 25, short: 5, long: 15, longEvery: 4, auto: false };
  const STORE_KEY = 'pomodoro.settings.v1';

  /* =========================================================
     DOM
     ========================================================= */
  const els = {
    body: document.body,
    dial: document.getElementById('dial'),
    ring: document.getElementById('ring'),
    time: document.getElementById('time'),
    sub: document.getElementById('sub'),
    modeLabel: document.getElementById('modeLabel'),
    primary: document.getElementById('primary'),
    reset: document.getElementById('reset'),
    skip: document.getElementById('skip'),
    dots: document.getElementById('dots'),
    settings: document.getElementById('settings'),
    settingsToggle: document.getElementById('settingsToggle'),
    toast: document.getElementById('toast'),
    flash: document.getElementById('flash'),
    status: document.getElementById('status')
  };

  /* =========================================================
     Helpers
     ========================================================= */
  const reduceMotionQuery = window.matchMedia
    ? window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')
    : null;

  function prefersReducedMotion() {
    return !!(reduceMotionQuery && reduceMotionQuery.matches);
  }

  function clamp(value, min, max) {
    return Math.min(max, Math.max(min, value));
  }

  function formatTime(ms) {
    const totalSeconds = Math.ceil(Math.max(0, ms) / 1000);
    const minutes = Math.floor(totalSeconds / 60);
    const seconds = totalSeconds % 60;
    return String(minutes).padStart(2, '0') + ':' + String(seconds).padStart(2, '0');
  }

  /* =========================================================
     Settings persistence
     ========================================================= */
  function loadSettings() {
    const out = Object.assign({}, DEFAULTS);
    let raw = null;

    try {
      raw = localStorage.getItem(STORE_KEY);
    } catch (err) {
      return out; // storage blocked (private mode, etc.)
    }
    if (!raw) return out;

    try {
      const saved = JSON.parse(raw);
      if (!saved || typeof saved !== 'object') return out;

      ['work', 'short', 'long', 'longEvery'].forEach(function (key) {
        const value = Number(saved[key]);
        if (Number.isFinite(value)) {
          out[key] = clamp(Math.round(value), LIMITS[key][0], LIMITS[key][1]);
        }
      });
      if (typeof saved.auto === 'boolean') out.auto = saved.auto;
    } catch (err) {
      /* corrupt payload — keep defaults */
    }
    return out;
  }

  function saveSettings() {
    try {
      localStorage.setItem(STORE_KEY, JSON.stringify(state.settings));
    } catch (err) {
      /* storage unavailable — settings simply won't persist */
    }
  }

  /* =========================================================
     State
     ========================================================= */
  const state = {
    settings: loadSettings(),
    mode: 'work',        // 'work' | 'short' | 'long'
    completed: 0,        // finished focus sessions
    remaining: 0,        // ms left in the current interval
    endAt: 0,            // absolute timestamp the interval ends at
    running: false,
    intervalId: null
  };

  function durationFor(mode) {
    return Math.round(state.settings[mode] * 60000);
  }

  state.remaining = durationFor('work');

  /* =========================================================
     Rendering
     ========================================================= */
  let lastTitle = '';

  function subText() {
    const perSet = state.settings.longEvery;
    if (state.mode === 'work') {
      return 'Session ' + ((state.completed % perSet) + 1) + ' of ' + perSet;
    }
    const c = state.completed;
    if (c === 0) return 'Break time';
    return c + (c === 1 ? ' session done' : ' sessions done');
  }

  function renderDots() {
    const count = state.settings.longEvery;

    if (els.dots.children.length !== count) {
      els.dots.replaceChildren();
      for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
        const dot = document.createElement('span');
        dot.className = 'dot';
        els.dots.appendChild(dot);
      }
    }

    // While the long break is running, the whole set reads as complete.
    const filled = state.mode === 'long' ? count : state.completed % count;
    const children = els.dots.children;
    for (let i = 0; i < children.length; i++) {
      children[i].classList.toggle('filled', i < filled);
    }
  }

  function render() {
    const total = durationFor(state.mode);
    const remaining = Math.max(0, state.remaining);
    const fraction = total > 0 ? Math.min(1, remaining / total) : 0;
    const untouched = remaining >= total;

    els.time.textContent = formatTime(remaining);
    els.ring.style.strokeDashoffset = String(RING_CIRCUMFERENCE * (1 - fraction));
    els.modeLabel.textContent = MODE_LABEL[state.mode];
    els.sub.textContent = subText();

    const label = state.running ? 'Pause' : (untouched ? 'Start' : 'Resume');
    if (els.primary.textContent !== label) els.primary.textContent = label;
    els.primary.setAttribute(
      'aria-label',
      label + ' ' + MODE_LABEL[state.mode].toLowerCase() + ' timer'
    );

    renderDots();

    const title = state.running
      ? formatTime(remaining) + ' · ' + MODE_LABEL[state.mode]
      : (untouched ? 'Pomodoro' : 'Paused · ' + formatTime(remaining));

    if (title !== lastTitle) {
      document.title = title;
      lastTitle = title;
    }
  }

  /* =========================================================
     Timer loop
     ========================================================= */
  function startLoop() {
    if (state.intervalId !== null) return;
    state.intervalId = window.setInterval(tick, 200);
  }

  function stopLoop() {
    if (state.intervalId !== null) {
      window.clearInterval(state.intervalId);
      state.intervalId = null;
    }
  }

  function tick() {
    if (!state.running) {
      stopLoop();
      return;
    }

    const left = state.endAt - Date.now();

    if (left <= 0) {
      state.remaining = 0;
      render();
      advance({ autostart: state.settings.auto });
      return;
    }

    state.remaining = left;
    render();
  }

  /* =========================================================
     Controls
     ========================================================= */
  function start() {
    if (state.running) return;

    const total = durationFor(state.mode);
    if (state.remaining <= 0 || state.remaining > total) state.remaining = total;

    state.endAt = Date.now() + state.remaining;
    state.running = true;
    startLoop();
    render();
  }

  function pause() {
    if (!state.running) return;
    state.remaining = Math.max(0, state.endAt - Date.now());
    state.running = false;
    stopLoop();
    render();
  }

  function toggle() {
    if (state.running) pause();
    else start();
  }

  function reset() {
    stopLoop();
    state.running = false;
    state.endAt = 0;
    state.remaining = durationFor(state.mode);
    render();
  }

  function skip() {
    advance({ autostart: state.settings.auto, silent: true });
  }

  function setMode(mode, options) {
    const opts = options || {};

    stopLoop();
    state.running = false;
    state.endAt = 0;
    state.mode = mode;
    state.remaining = durationFor(mode);

    if (els.body.dataset.mode !== mode) els.body.dataset.mode = mode;

    render();
    if (opts.autostart) start();
  }

  /**
   * Move to the next interval in the cycle.
   * Focus → (long break every N sessions, otherwise short break) → Focus
   */
  function advance(options) {
    const opts = options || {};
    const finished = state.mode;

    if (finished === 'work') state.completed += 1;

    const perSet = Math.max(1, state.settings.longEvery);
    const next = finished === 'work'
      ? (state.completed % perSet === 0 ? 'long' : 'short')
      : 'work';

    if (!opts.silent) celebrate(finished, next, !!opts.autostart);

    setMode(next, { autostart: opts.autostart });
  }

  /* =========================================================
     Visual alerts
     ========================================================= */
  function showToast(message) {
    els.toast.textContent = message;
    els.toast.classList.add('show');
    window.clearTimeout(showToast.timerId);
    showToast.timerId = window.setTimeout(function () {
      els.toast.classList.remove('show');
    }, prefersReducedMotion() ? 6500 : 4200);
  }

  function flashScreen() {
    if (prefersReducedMotion()) return;
    els.flash.classList.remove('on');
    void els.flash.offsetWidth; // force reflow so the animation restarts
    els.flash.classList.add('on');
  }

  function pulseDial() {
    if (prefersReducedMotion()) return;
    els.dial.classList.remove('pulse');
    void els.dial.offsetWidth;
    els.dial.classList.add('pulse');
  }

  function celebrate(finished, next, willAutoStart) {
    const message = finished === 'work'
      ? (next === 'long'
          ? 'Focus complete — long break earned.'
          : 'Focus complete — take a short break.')
      : 'Break over — back to focus.';

    showToast(message);
    flashScreen();
    pulseDial();

    els.status.textContent =
      MODE_LABEL[finished] + ' finished. ' +
      MODE_LABEL[next] + ', ' + formatTime(durationFor(next)) + '.' +
      (willAutoStart ? ' Started automatically.' : '');
  }

  /* =========================================================
     Settings UI
     ========================================================= */
  function applySetting(key, value) {
    if (state.settings[key] === value) return;
    state.settings[key] = value;
    saveSettings();

    if (key === 'longEvery') {
      renderDots();
    } else if (key === state.mode && !state.running) {
      // Changing the length of the current, paused interval restarts it.
      state.remaining = durationFor(state.mode);
    }
    render();
  }

  function wireNumberField(inputId, key) {
    const input = document.getElementById(inputId);
    const min = LIMITS[key][0];
    const max = LIMITS[key][1];
    input.value = String(state.settings[key]);

    input.addEventListener('input', function () {
      const value = Number(input.value);
      // Ignore empty / out-of-range while the user is still typing.
      if (!Number.isFinite(value) || value < min || value > max) return;
      applySetting(key, Math.round(value));
    });

    input.addEventListener('change', function () {
      const raw = Number(input.value);
      const safe = Number.isFinite(raw)
        ? clamp(Math.round(raw), min, max)
        : state.settings[key];
      input.value = String(safe);
      applySetting(key, safe);
    });
  }

  function setSettingsOpen(open) {
    els.settings.hidden = !open;
    els.settingsToggle.setAttribute('aria-expanded', String(open));
    els.settingsToggle.textContent = open ? 'Done' : 'Settings';
  }

  /* =========================================================
     Init
     ========================================================= */
  els.body.dataset.mode = state.mode;
  els.ring.style.strokeDasharray = String(RING_CIRCUMFERENCE);

  wireNumberField('set-work', 'work');
  wireNumberField('set-short', 'short');
  wireNumberField('set-long', 'long');
  wireNumberField('set-every', 'longEvery');

  const autoInput = document.getElementById('set-auto');
  autoInput.checked = state.settings.auto;
  autoInput.addEventListener('change', function () {
    state.settings.auto = autoInput.checked;
    saveSettings();
  });

  els.settings.addEventListener('submit', function (event) {
    event.preventDefault();
  });

  els.settingsToggle.addEventListener('click', function () {
    setSettingsOpen(els.settings.hidden);
  });

  els.primary.addEventListener('click', toggle);
  els.reset.addEventListener('click', reset);
  els.skip.addEventListener('click', skip);

  /* Keyboard shortcuts — skipped while typing, and Space is left alone on
     buttons so the focused button's own behaviour still works. */
  document.addEventListener('keydown', function (event) {
    if (event.repeat || event.metaKey || event.ctrlKey || event.altKey) return;

    const target = event.target;
    const tag = target && target.tagName;
    const isTyping = tag === 'INPUT' || tag === 'TEXTAREA' || tag === 'SELECT' ||
                     (target && target.isContentEditable);
    if (isTyping) return;

    if (event.code === 'Space') {
      if (tag === 'BUTTON') return;
      event.preventDefault();
      toggle();
    } else if (event.key === 'r' || event.key === 'R') {
      reset();
    } else if (event.key === 's' || event.key === 'S') {
      skip();
    } else if (event.key === 'Escape' && !els.settings.hidden) {
      setSettingsOpen(false);
    }
  });

  /* Resync immediately when the tab becomes visible again (background
     intervals get throttled, but we always compute from a real timestamp). */
  document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', function () {
    if (!document.hidden && state.running) tick();
  });

  render();
})();
</script>
</body>
</html>
```
### How the Timer Flows and Feels

This timer runs as a small state machine: each interval is either work, a short break, or a long break. The dial ring, accent color, and session dots update with every phase.

- **Start/pause/reset/skip** — the primary button toggles between Start, Resume, and Pause. Reset restarts the current interval; Skip jumps to the next one without changing completed counts.
- **Configurable rhythm** — open Settings to set focus, short break, and long break minutes, plus how many focus sessions trigger a long break. Changes to the current paused interval apply immediately.
- **Visual alerts** — when a phase ends, the card pulses, the screen flashes in the accent color, and a toast message appears. If auto-start is enabled, the next interval begins right away.
- **Responsive feedback** — the ring drains as time passes, session dots fill, and the browser tab title shows remaining time. Keyboard shortcuts (Space, R, S) work when focus isn’t in a settings field.
---

**Optimization Tip:** You can change the default interval lengths, long-break frequency, and auto-start setting in the `DEFAULTS` object near the top of the script — for example, `work: 25` or `longEvery: 4`.

  • 5 ui-practicality: Responsive layout with clamp/fluid sizing, clear mode label and session dots, visual completion alerts (flash, pulse, toast), keyboard shortcuts, aria-live status, and reduced-motion support.

```html
        </div>
```
